People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

If I’m sitting with someone, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m clicking a pen, messing with my keys… half the time I don’t even notice I’m doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I cut people off a lot, mostly my mom or the counselor. Like, something pops into my head and I just say it before they’re even done talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble completing tasks or assignments at work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I’ve missed some training deadlines, and I’ve had to redo applications because I didn’t finish them right or on time. It’s kind of kept me from moving forward with becoming an automotive technician, even though I think I’d be okay with the actual hands-on part.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Mostly with my mom or my counselor. I’ll have an answer in my head and just say it before they’re done talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or staying on task in your daily life outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside of that too. Like I’ll put on a repair video or try to read a tool manual, and a few minutes later I realize I’ve just been staring at it. I have to go back because none of it really stayed in my head.\n\nDoctor: Were you also fidgety or restless when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. In elementary school I was always getting moved around in class… teachers said I talked out of turn, forgot homework, or rushed through worksheets and made dumb mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms consistently since you were a kid, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of been there since I was younger, but it got really disruptive about… almost two years ago. Like a year and ten months, after my soccer injury messed up my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focus and staying on task is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the focus part is more of the problem. I just get pulled off by anything — like a car outside, my phone, or even some random thought — and then I’m doing something else before I really notice.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity caused you significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… daily life, mostly. Which is frustrating, because I know I can do the hands-on part.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity in multiple settings, such as at home, with friends, or in different jobs?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. If I’m sitting with someone, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m clicking a pen, messing with my keys… half the time I don’t even notice I’m doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I cut people off a lot, mostly my mom or the counselor. Like, something pops into my head and I just say it before they’re even done talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble completing tasks or assignments at work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I’ve missed some training deadlines, and I’ve had to redo applications because I didn’t finish them right or on time. It’s kind of kept me from moving forward with becoming an automotive technician, even though I think I’d be okay with the actual hands-on part.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Mostly with my mom or my counselor. I’ll have an answer in my head and just say it before they’re done talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or staying on task in your daily life outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside of that too. Like I’ll put on a repair video or try to read a tool manual, and a few minutes later I realize I’ve just been staring at it. I have to go back because none of it really stayed in my head.\n\nDoctor: Were you also fidgety or restless when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. In elementary school I was always getting moved around in class… teachers said I talked out of turn, forgot homework, or rushed through worksheets and made dumb mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms consistently since you were a kid, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of been there since I was younger, but it got really disruptive about… almost two years ago. Like a year and ten months, after my soccer injury messed up my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focus and staying on task is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the focus part is more of the problem. I just get pulled off by anything — like a car outside, my phone, or even some random thought — and then I’m doing something else before I really notice.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity caused you significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… daily life, mostly. I’ve missed training deadlines and had to redo applications a few times, so I’m still kind of stuck instead of moving forward toward being an automotive technician. Which is frustrating, because I know I can do the hands-on part.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity in multiple settings, such as at home, with friends, or in different jobs?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean… it’s not just one place, I guess, but I haven’t really kept track of it like that. It comes up in normal stuff day to day.\n\nDoctor: Were these difficulties with focus and restlessness present before you were 12 years old?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. In elementary school, teachers used to move my seat a lot because I’d talk when I wasn’t supposed to. I’d forget homework too, and I’d rush through worksheets and make dumb mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity caused you significant problems in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I’m not really sure. I haven’t thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced problems with friends or family because of your difficulties with focus, restlessness, or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I guess I cut people off a lot, like my mom or my counselor. An answer comes into my head and I just say it before they’re finished. If I’m sitting with someone, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m clicking a pen, messing with my keys… half the time I don’t even notice I’m doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I cut people off a lot, mostly my mom or the counselor. I’ve missed some training deadlines, and I’ve had to redo applications because I didn’t finish them right or on time. It’s kind of kept me from moving forward with becoming an automotive technician, even though I think I’d be okay with the actual hands-on part.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Mostly with my mom or my counselor. I’ll have an answer in my head and just say it before they’re done talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or staying on task in your daily life outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside of that too. Like I’ll put on a repair video or try to read a tool manual, and a few minutes later I realize I’ve just been staring at it. In elementary school I was always getting moved around in class… teachers said I talked out of turn, forgot homework, or rushed through worksheets and made dumb mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms consistently since you were a kid, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of been there since I was younger, but it got really disruptive about… almost two years ago. Like a year and ten months, after my soccer injury messed up my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focus and staying on task is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the focus part is more of the problem.
